# R19-ACCEPT-A: acceptance runs and evidence

## Dispatch (coordinator, 2026-11-11)

Round lineage: R19-ACCEPT, split into A (runs and evidence, this round) and B (matrix closure and the
first Linux gate on the per-directory layout). The split keeps each independent review bounded: A
reviews run evidence and any product defects it exposes, B reviews record closure and the platform gate.

Starting revision: `91e0df24f2167471e49ec057c7159bce5dff3608` on `dev/planar-v2-vernacular`. The tree is
clean at dispatch; everything this round produces is uncommitted until approval. Pre-existing changes: none.
Candidate identity comes only from the workflow identity tool run from the repository root.

### Objective

Operator answered Q17 (2026-11-11): the real R19 spec is the approved R19-CMAKE task, and v5's
"previously run on old Planar" deviation is accepted. This round produces the live acceptance runs for
R19 (`roadmap.md` R19 acceptance row, `execution-design.md` "R19: single-root external-driver
acceptance", `implementation.md` R19), using a source-built candidate and fresh scratch ledgers only.

1. Quota fixture in both groupings (multi-unit dependencies) driven through the external driver against a
   fresh ledger. No quota spec fixture is present in the repository (existing "quota" hits are storage
   quotas), so the coder must author it as test-only workspace input and record where it came from. [C02 AMENDMENT D2: reused fixtures/render/quota/ unmodified; see the C02 section]
2. Fresh-ledger rerun of the R19-CMAKE job with injected faults: an interrupted check, a process restart,
   `check-recover`, takeover, and one rejecting verdict, so repair and `max_rounds`/blockers are exercised
   before final approval. Reuse the driver and check scaffolding under `tests/native/r19-cmake-support/`.
3. Inspect accepted declarations, dispatch, and state/check/verdict references through restart and faults
   with `history --event` and `--at-event`, without manual database edits or re-execution of checks or Lua.
4. The reference orchestrator must render usable guidance against the live APIs during these runs.
5. Address or explicitly carry: the R19-CMAKE nonblocking reviewer-packet wording (the check section says
   no receipt exists before the observations list passing checks); the whole-engine captured-preview
   instruction limit.

### Must not do

No product change unless a run exposes a real defect; such a defect gets a red-first behavioural
regression test (exact command and hash of the pre-fix snapshot) and keeps every closed-table diagnostic
byte-identical. No scheduler, model calls, or checkout lifecycle in Planar. No A21 composition and no
controlled A34 experiments. No touching `~/.planar`, no installed or legacy binaries, no manual database
edits, no hand-computed hashes. The preexisting accounting audit and configure timestamp follow-up stay
carried unless touched. Boundary pin rule: if `runtime.cpp` or production-embedded Lua changes, re-measure
and re-pin `c02_off_boundary` in `src/engine/runtime/external_registration.t.cpp` after the last such
edit, measuring Linux in the container. Do not run any script under `tests/native/` other than the
reused R19 support driver the coder owns; reviewers use only the workflow identity tool, copied to
scratchpad first.

### Owned files

New or extended support under `tests/native/r19-accept-support/` and evidence under
`tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/` (reuse of `r19-cmake-support/` by copy or import, which stays
unmodified). Product source files are owned only if a defect is exposed, and then are listed here by the
repair. The coordinator owns this record. The matrix, roadmap and `acceptance-matrix.md` are not edited
in A; B owns closure. Every edited file must be listed in the coder report.

### Acceptance criteria

- Quota fixture runs complete in both groupings with raw responses retained per action, byte-identical
  replay after restart, and exact-target independent approval through the CLI.
- The faulted CMake rerun shows each injected fault with its observed recovery, one rejecting verdict
  followed by repair, and a max_rounds/blocker exercise, then final approval, with no checks re-executed
  on replay and no ledger writes from inspection.
- Raw logs kept, redaction passing, no host paths in committed evidence, only src and committed data
  files hash-pinned (never other records, reviews, REPORTs or the roadmap).
- Every new test or check can fail; the coder names the mutant each should kill and the prober applies
  mutants in a scratch copy outside the checkout. A surviving mutant stays a survivor.
- Focused existing gates for any touched module pass with nonzero test counts and zero skips; a full
  Debug gate runs if product code changed.

### Agreed verification

Strict wave order: edits, gate, probes, record sync, commit. Fresh scratch ledger roots per run; source-built
candidate binary by explicit path. Opus independent review; approval commits with explicit paths,
`git add -f` for gitignored evidence, `git commit -s`, no co-author trailer, never raw logs. Afterward the
roadmap opening and Next work are updated. R19-ACCEPT-B then covers A24 applicability, matrix closure
with evidence pointers, minimal A34 provenance, and the Linux gate.

## Repair pass 1 (mutation survivors)

Test-only repair; no product or support-library code changed. Edited files:
`tests/native/r19-accept-support/accept_test.py` (new class `AcceptLibGapTest`,
11 tests; `support_copy()` now also copies `cmake_check.py` and `accept_lib.py`
into the copied support tree, fixing the I25 and I35 baseline failures, which
were a test layout defect, not a contract conflict) and
`tests/native/r19-accept-support/acceptance_cli_test.py` (two new tests in
`AcceptanceCliTest`), plus this record. No existing test name was removed or
renamed. Mutants each new test must kill: I3b, I10, I10c, I12, I12c, I12d, I13b,
I17c, I24, I24b, I29b, I30c, I35b, E6, E8, E10, E11, E13, E14, E15, E17, E18
(docstrings name them). Messages asserted for E6/E8/E10/E11 so a different
check failing cannot mask the mutant. Repair pass 1 figures (superseded by the C01 figures below, which the final
R19-ACCEPT-A-C02 section supersedes in turn): `accept_test` 47/47 pass; `acceptance_cli_test` 9 tests, only A6 fails
(needs PLANAR_CANDIDATE, as before). The prober had to re-run the mutants; none were applied here.
The survivors of the first probe were later closed in C01.

## Dispatch R19-ACCEPT-A-C01 (coordinator, 2026-11-11)

Corrective for R19-ACCEPT-A, which returned gate-failed. All repository gates passed (1851/1851 on macOS
Debug and Release, Linux arm64 Debug and Release, with no Dockerfile change on the per-directory layout),
but acceptance was not met: the live quota combined run stopped on a candidate product defect.
`history --event 6` on a `check_recorded` event exits 4 with
`{"error":{"code":"internal","message":"Unsupported trusted structural value"}}`. The message originates
in `src/engine/runtime/runtime.cpp`. Evidence is in `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/` (run
`quota-combined-defect1`, raw directory). Starting revision remains
`91e0df24f2167471e49ec057c7159bce5dff3608`; the tree is uncommitted and carries the whole R19-ACCEPT-A
work (this record, `tests/native/r19-accept-support/`, and the gitignored evidence directories
`r19-accept-a-evidence` and `r19-accept-evidence`). Build on it; never discard it. Pre-existing changes:
exactly that uncommitted work. No operator question is open (Q17 answered).

### Scope, in strict wave order

1. Reproduce and diagnose the history defect from the evidence. Write a red-first Catch2 regression test
   in the owning module, record the failing behavioural run (exact command, hash of the pre-fix snapshot)
   and keep that log; then fix product code minimally and confirm no closed-table diagnostic text changes.
   If `runtime.cpp` or production-embedded Lua changes, re-pin `c02_off_boundary` in
   `src/engine/runtime/external_registration.t.cpp` after the last such edit (Linux measured in the
   container). If diagnosis shows the behavior is contract-correct and the driver is wrong, fix the driver
   and record why.
2. Complete the remaining live runs from R19-ACCEPT-A against fresh scratch ledgers with the fixed
   source-built candidate: quota combined and split groupings; the mini-fault run; the heavy faulted CMake
   rerun (interrupted check, restart, check-recover, takeover, one rejecting verdict, repair,
   max_rounds/blockers, final approval); and the limits, packet, index and scan steps. Keep raw output per
   action, byte-identical replay, no manual database edits, and no re-execution on inspection.
3. Kill the 22 surviving mutants from the R19-ACCEPT-A probes by adding negative fixtures: accept_lib.py
   I3b, I10, I10c, I12, I12c, I12d, I13b, I24, I24b, I30c, I35b, E13, E14, E15, E17, E18; cmake_check.py
   I17c; check_runner.py I29b; run_acceptance.py E6, E8, E10, E11 (these failed only because baseline A6
   was red). A6 must run green with `PLANAR_CANDIDATE` set. Provide red-first records for the 14
   repair-pass tests where feasible, and state honestly which lack one.
4. Wire or explicitly run the Python support suites in the gate driver and report their counts, separate
   from ctest's 1851.

A24 applicability and matrix closure may remain for R19-ACCEPT-B unless the runs complete early; the
report must say which. Carried unless touched: reviewer-packet wording, captured-preview instruction
limit, accounting audit, configure timestamp.

### Must not do

No scheduler, model calls or checkout lifecycle. No A21 composition, no A34 experiments. Never touch
`~/.planar`; never run installed or legacy planar binaries; all ledgers in fresh scratch roots. Do not
edit the matrix, roadmap or `acceptance-matrix.md` (R19-ACCEPT-B closes them). Keep every existing
diagnostic message byte-identical and never remove or rename an existing test. Coders never mutate
checkout files; only the mutation prober applies mutants, in a scratch copy outside the checkout; a
survivor stays a survivor. Do not add public fields or methods to product types only for observability.

### Owned files

`src/engine/runtime/runtime.cpp` and its colocated test only if the diagnosis finds a product defect
(plus `external_registration.t.cpp` for a re-pin, and any production Lua actually touched, to be listed by
the coder); `tests/native/r19-accept-support/*`; `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/*`;
`tests/native/r19-accept-a-evidence/*`; the gate driver wiring for the Python suites; this record.
`tests/native/r19-cmake-support/` stays unmodified. Every edited file must be listed in the coder report.

### Acceptance criteria

- The history defect has a red-first behavioural regression test with a kept failing log, a minimal fix
  (or a recorded driver-only fix), and unchanged diagnostics.
- Quota combined and split runs complete with raw responses per action, byte-identical replay after
  restart and exact-target independent approval through the CLI. The faulted CMake rerun shows each
  injected fault with its observed recovery, a rejecting verdict then repair, a max_rounds/blocker
  exercise, then final approval, with no check re-executed on replay and no ledger writes from inspection.
- All 22 listed mutants are killed by re-probe, with kill names naming the new fixtures; A6 green.
- Python suite counts reported; ctest gate nonzero with zero skips (full Debug and Release plus the Linux
  container gate if product code changed).
- Record rule: records and evidence pin sha256 only for src and committed evidence data files, never other
  records, reviews, REPORTs or the roadmap. Redaction passes; no host paths in committed evidence.

### Agreed verification

Wave order: edits, gate, probes, record sync, commit. Opus independent review. Reviewers and coders run
only the workflow identity tool (copied to scratchpad), never scripts under `tests/native/`, except the
support driver the coder owns. On approval: commit R19-ACCEPT-A plus this corrective per
`orchestration.md` (explicit paths, `git add -f` for gitignored evidence, `git commit -s`, no co-author
trailer; never raw logs, never a file failing redaction, never superseded plan copies containing host
paths), then update the roadmap opening and Next work.

### Defect, fix and repairs

- Defect: `history --event N` on a `check_recorded` event exited 4 `Unsupported trusted structural value`. The
  producer was `history_rows` in `storage.cpp`, which set both `integer` and `boolean` on every `n` or `b`
  scalar; a nonzero value gave an integer node with `boolean = true` (and a true flag a boolean node with
  `integer = 1`), which the runtime's structural-value check (`runtime.cpp:208-213`) correctly refuses. The
  refusal is contract-correct and stays; no diagnostic text changed.
- Red-first: `red-c01/history/RED.md`, pre-fix snapshot (`identity.py src`, combined row)
  `b5b9a3fb357c2ded89b7deb8ff5ba5550dc5d1fd359c22ba2e28099ca1bf4ec4`; command
  `ctest --test-dir build/rewrite-debug -R "R19-ACCEPT-A-C01 history event of a recorded check" --no-tests=error --output-on-failure`,
  exit 8, 2 of 3 failed (H1 and H2, assertion lines in `execution.t.cpp`), H3 passed unfixed (a guard against
  over-correction). `validate_red_record` ACCEPTED.
- Repairs after review and probe findings (each kept its superseded copy, nothing deleted): the plan was revised
  to revision 4 (`plan/superseded-1..3`); gate run 1 failed redaction on a host-path string in an `accept_test.py`
  fixture and was repeated; gate run 2 ran its first Python step without the candidate (exit 1) and repeated it
  with the authoring-stack binary; the first re-probe found three survivors, closed by new fixtures (below);
  gate run 3 and run 4 then passed on identical bytes. Driver and test expectations were corrected where the
  contract, not the candidate, was right (REPORT.md section 6; the citations were verified only through the
  coders' records).
- Amendment to the earlier owned-files constraint: `src/engine/ledger/storage.cpp` and
  `src/engine/ledger/execution.t.cpp` were added to the owned files (decision D1, authority conflict D31,
  stage-0 coordinator amendment). Reason: the defect is in the R17/R18 history expansion, not in `runtime.cpp`
  as the dispatch text assumed. The "fresh corrective round of the owning step" wording of
  `execution-design.md` is satisfied by this corrective round; B should confirm.

All sixteen gate commands met their criteria (`REPORT.md` section 2).
- ctest Debug and Release: 1854 of 1854 passed, zero skips; `rewrite-check.py gate` "Executed 1854 registered
  tests; zero skips" for both; no-change builds `ninja: no work to do.`
- Test inventory: 1851 before, 1854 after; no name removed, three added, all in `execution.t.cpp`:
  `R19-ACCEPT-A-C01 history event of a recorded check keeps evidence integer nodes free of the boolean flag`,
  `... keeps evidence boolean nodes free of the integer value`,
  `... preserves evidence integer and boolean values exactly`. No test was renamed.
- Python support suites (`unittest discover`, `PLANAR_CANDIDATE` set, scratch HOME and PLANAR_DB): 108 tests,

### Probe results (C01 re-probe, `tests/native/r19-accept-a-c01-evidence/probes/PROBES.md`)

Mutants were applied only in a scratch copy outside the checkout, restored and hash-verified after each.
- All 22 mutants listed in this corrective's scope are KILLED, each by a named new fixture: I3b, I10, I10c,
  I12, I12c, I12d, I13b, I24, I24b, I30c, I35b, E13, E14, E15, E17, E18 (`accept_lib.py`), I17c
  (`cmake_check.py`), I29b (`check_runner.py`), E6, E8, E10, E11 (`run_acceptance.py`; these had failed only
  because baseline A6 was red). A6 is green with `PLANAR_CANDIDATE` (62 tests for the two unit suites) and
  still fails, never skips, without it. M-I10d (both interruption requirements dropped together) is killed.
- History rows H1, H2, H3a, H3b, H3c, H-prefix and H5 are KILLED (carried from the first probe run because
  `storage.cpp` and `execution.t.cpp` are byte-identical); S1, S2, S3, R1, R2 and their variants are KILLED.
- Earlier survivors and why they survived: M-I13b2 (no fixture gave a capped rejection a nonempty `repair`
  list); S2b and S2c each survived alone because the two refusals mask each other (a zero-test module also
  exits nonzero), while S2bc was killed; L3b (the quota replay had no counter tamper, so the abort path was
  unreached). Closed by `AcceptLibGapTest.test_trajectory_capped_rejection_requires_repair_not_admitted`
  (new assertion), the new `AcceptLibGapTest.test_suite_refusals_each_clause_stands_alone`, and the new
  `QuotaLiveTest.test_run_grouping_aborts_when_replay_reexecutes_a_check` with the new `tamper.counter` key in
  `quota_driver.py`; all three re-ran KILLED, and X-Q14a proves the new fixture can fail. No survivor remained
  among the C01 rows run or carried (C01 scope only; the C02 probes of the F, L and Q rows ended with two survivors, F11-b and L17, both KILLED in C03, see the C03 section).
- Limits of the probe: driver rows L2, L6, L7, L9, L10, L12 to L19, Q1 to Q19 and F1 to F25 beyond the L rows
  that were probed were NOT mutation-probed; the heavy CMake run was not probed; the full live baseline was not
  re-run by the prober (the gate counts above are the live baseline).

### Live run results

- Quota combined, quota split and quota regroup (combined to split from the same spec bytes) completed against
  fresh scratch ledgers, `status: accepted`, with the history fix in the recorded candidate: combined 10
  events and 3 check runs, split 15 events and 6 check runs, regroup 10 events and 3 check runs; raw argv,
  stdout, stderr and exit per action under `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/raw/quota-*-c01/raw/`, one
  `result.json` each. The earlier run on the pre-fix candidate (`quota-combined-defect1`) stays as defect
  evidence.
- Not executed: the mini-fault run, the heavy faulted CMake rerun (interrupted check, restart, `check-recover`,
  takeover, rejecting verdict then repair, `max_rounds` blocker, final approval), and the `limits`, `packet`,
  `index`, `scan` and `verify-redaction` steps. `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/runs/` holds only the
  defect-run index and `limits/` is empty. The fault behaviours are observed only through the 25
  `FaultLiveTest` tests, which drive the mini fixture live inside the gate; no retained run directory or
  `rerun-index` output exists for them. The reviewer-packet wording and the whole-engine captured-preview limit
  stay carried, untouched.

### Remaining limits and carried items

- Mini-fault, heavy CMake rerun and the packet/limits/index/scan/verify-redaction steps remain to be run with
  the recorded candidate; they are carried, with the exact limit that no run directory exists for them today.
- Not red-first (stated): `QuotaLiveTest.test_split_grouping_retains_raw_exact_targets_and_non_builder_actor`
  (absent before the edit; AttributeError only), the 14 repair-pass tests (no red possible), H3, H5 and the
  guard rows listed `false`. `red-c01/rf/RED.md` still says PROBE-1 "has not run"; it is a point-in-time
  record superseded by `probes/PROBES.md`. The older `red/RED.md` pass records L13, L16, L17, L19 as crashes
  against a non-candidate binary; the D39 records supersede it.
- Gate limits: Linux arm64 only; ctest and Python names compared only against run 1 and the previous round's
  inventory (no committed list exists after R15); the G1 `comm -23` check has no registered script and was done
  manually.
- Accounting audit and configure timestamp follow-up stay carried and untouched. The D4 deviation (the R19-CMAKE
  task run as the non-gating live run was scripted rather than a Claude Code session) goes to B for confirmation.

| N2 | Quota replay aborts with AcceptError when a replay re-executes a check (counter increase), so the counter comparison is guarded | quota_driver.py replay: ignore the check-execution counter comparison (L3b); tamper never applied (X-Q14a) | `QuotaLiveTest.test_run_grouping_aborts_when_replay_reexecutes_a_check` in `tests/native/r19-accept-support/quota_live_test.py` (new `tamper.counter` key in quota_driver.py) | false (closes survivor L3b) | no red possible: test added after the red stage to kill a survivor; its red is the probe kill | L3b KILLED (re-run); X-Q14a KILLED (re-run) |
| N3 | A capped rejection must carry no repair roles (extra assertion `repair=["builder"]` inside the existing test; no new test name) | accept_lib.py _capped_rejection: repair roles allowed on a capped rejection (M-I13b2) | `AcceptLibGapTest.test_trajectory_capped_rejection_requires_repair_not_admitted` in `tests/native/r19-accept-support/accept_test.py` | false (closes survivor M-I13b2) | no red possible: test added after the red stage to kill a survivor; its red is the probe kill | M-I13b KILLED (re-run); M-I13b2 KILLED (re-run) |

Decision: request-changes ([review](R19-ACCEPT-A-C01-review.md)). The history defect fix, the quota
both-groupings live acceptance and the 22-mutant re-probe stand as recorded above, but the round is not approved.

`6612206823f3350ea5e9ce36bf9f39750a553894ca6624941379f2b200dd9891`, identical to the reviewed candidate.
candidateCurrent: true. Nothing was committed.

  README. Fix: one exact committable list without raw trees, redact or regenerate listed files, run the

  outcome ("needs a live scenario"). Fix: probe them in a scratch copy and record each kill by test name.

## Dispatch R19-ACCEPT-A-C02 (coordinator, 2026-11-11)

Second corrective for R19-ACCEPT-A, answering the three blocking findings (R1, R2, R3) and five advisories
(A1 to A5) of the [C01 review](R19-ACCEPT-A-C01-review.md), which returned request-changes. This is the
second request-changes in the lineage (the first was the gate-failed original round, which is not counted).
The C01 candidate is kept whole: the history fix in `src/engine/ledger/storage.cpp`, its three regression
tests in `src/engine/ledger/execution.t.cpp`, the quota live runs and the 22-mutant re-probe all stand.

Starting revision: `91e0df24f2167471e49ec057c7159bce5dff3608` on `dev/planar-v2-vernacular`. The tree is
uncommitted and carries the whole R19-ACCEPT-A and C01 work. Pre-existing changes: `roadmap.md` (status
edit), the two `src/engine/ledger/` files, this record, the C01 review, `tests/native/r19-accept-support/`
and the gitignored evidence folders. Candidate identity at dispatch (combined sha256
`6612206823f3350ea5e9ce36bf9f39750a553894ca6624941379f2b200dd9891`, tracked src diff
`83a16fdee033fce5a1a792d8d65f4090fba8dacffc2d38013684cacb4daeba19`) is unchanged; no operator question is open.

### Objective, in strict order

1. R1 (first and most important; no later item starts until it is done). Execute, with the recorded C01
   candidate (`planar_authoring_stack` built from the current `src`, explicit path, recorded through
   `accept_lib.require_recorded_candidate`), each on a fresh scratch ledger with raw argv, stdout, stderr and
   exit per action, no manual database edits and no re-execution on inspection or replay: the mini-fault
   run; the heavy faulted CMake rerun (interrupted check, process restart, `check-recover`, takeover, one
   rejecting verdict, repair, `max_rounds` and blockers, final approval); and the `limits`, `packet`,
   `index`, `scan` and `verify-redaction` steps. Produce `runs/cmake-rerun/index.json` under
   `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/` through `run_acceptance.py rerun-index`, and satisfy invariants L20
   and L21. Long runs start in the background with a DONE marker and are awaited by repeated foreground
   waits; the coder never returns before DONE. If a run exposes a product defect, stop and report it with a
   red-first regression test proposal; do not paper over it and do not change `src/` without a
   coordinator decision.
2. R2. Build one exact committable file list: no raw trees, no superseded plan copies with host paths.
   Redact or regenerate every listed file with host paths (the identity-script path in the `red-c01`
   `RED.md` files, the `/var/folders` path in the driver `RED.md`, `inventory.txt`, `red/run_red.sh`).
   Reconcile this record's commit list with the P6 README's list, then run
   `scripts/redact-local-paths.py check` on exactly that list and record command, exit status and list.
3. R3. Mutation-probe the fault-path and quota invariant rows F1 to F25, L2, L6, L7, L9, L10, L12 to L19 and
   Q1 to Q19 in a scratch copy outside the checkout using the live `FaultLiveTest` and `QuotaLiveTest`
   (about 8 minutes per run), one raw log per mutant, each kill recorded by its named test. A survivor stays
   a survivor and gets a negative fixture; heavy-run rows are probed after R1.
4. Advisories: A1 supersede the stale REPORT statements (a note in the evidence README, since raw REPORTs
   are not edited); A2 capture the G1 before and after test-name lists under the invariant's path; A3 run
   the Python gate step through `run_acceptance.py suites`; A4 no `.pyc` in the support tree
   (`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, `-B`; remove the existing `__pycache__` that this round's tooling wrote);
   A5 amend objective item 1 of the first dispatch (the quota fixture reuses `fixtures/render/quota/`,
   decision D2) and record the D4 deviation and the `storage.cpp` owned-file amendment.

### Dependencies and must-not-do

Depends on the C01 candidate and on the committed R19-CMAKE support (`tests/native/r19-cmake-support/`,
unmodified). A24 applicability and matrix closure stay for R19-ACCEPT-B, with the carried items (reviewer
packet wording, captured-preview instruction limit, accounting audit, configure timestamp). Product code
(`src/`) must not change unless a run exposes a defect. Keep every closed-table diagnostic byte-identical,
never remove or rename an existing test, never delete raw logs, never touch `~/.planar`, never run
installed or legacy planar binaries, and never hand-write hashes (identity comes only from the workflow
identity tool). Coders never mutate checkout files or `build/rewrite-debug`; only the prober applies
mutants. Hash-pin rule: pin only `src` and committed evidence data files, never records, reviews, REPORTs
or the roadmap. Disk: about 40 GiB free; keep scratch copies small and remove only scratch build copies
this round created. Reviewers and coders run only the workflow identity tool (copied to scratchpad), never
scripts under `tests/native/`, except that the coder owns and runs the `r19-accept-support` drivers.

### Owned files

`tests/native/r19-accept-support/` (only where a run needs a driver or test fix, each fix listed);
`tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/`, `tests/native/r19-accept-a-evidence/`,
`tests/native/r19-accept-a-c01-evidence/` and the new `tests/native/r19-accept-a-c02-evidence/`; this
record. `src/engine/ledger/storage.cpp` and `execution.t.cpp` are already in the candidate and stay
unchanged unless a defect is exposed. Every edited file must be listed in the coder report.

### Acceptance criteria

- The mini-fault and heavy faulted CMake runs exist with raw per-action output, each injected fault with its
  observed recovery, a rejecting verdict then repair, a `max_rounds` blocker exercise, then final approval,
  no re-execution on replay and no ledger writes from inspection; `runs/cmake-rerun/index.json` exists and
  L20 and L21 hold; `limits`, `packet`, `index`, `scan` and `verify-redaction` are recorded.
- One exact committable list with no raw tree; redaction check exits 0 on exactly that list.
- Every F, L and Q row in scope has a probe outcome with the killing test named; survivors are reported as
  survivors with negative fixtures.
- Advisories A1 to A5 resolved or stated. Python suites reported separately from ctest; a ctest gate runs
  only if `src/` changes (then full Debug and Release and Linux as in C01).

### Agreed verification

Strict wave order: R1 runs, R2 list and redaction, R3 probes, record sync, Opus review, then commit
covering R19-ACCEPT-A, C01 and C02 (including the `storage.cpp` fix and its tests) with explicit paths,
`git add -f` for gitignored evidence, `git commit -s`, no co-author trailer, never raw logs or a file failing
redaction. Afterward the roadmap opening and Next work are updated.

  `FaultLiveTest.test_trace_is_derived_from_executed_steps_in_plain_cmake_run` both pass under the mutant, because the plain cmake run used by the fixture
  returns through `stopped_result` and never reaches the mutated line in `finish()`. The earlier expectation in `probes/FIX-NOTES.md` that the plain run
  discriminates the constant is superseded by this observation. A real fix needs a run that completes through `finish()` with an executed verb sequence unlike
  the 15-entry list, or a direct `finish()` unit call on a driver with a custom trace; none exists. Reported as survivors; not relabelled.

## C02 outcome: request-changes (2026-11-12)

Review: [R19-ACCEPT-A-C02-review.md](R19-ACCEPT-A-C02-review.md). The reviewed candidate is unchanged on disk: the identity tool
(`src tests/native/r19-accept-support`, HEAD `91e0df24f2167471e49ec057c7159bce5dff3608`) reports tracked sha256
`83a16fdee033fce5a1a792d8d65f4090fba8dacffc2d38013684cacb4daeba19` and combined sha256
`988e085f2272f80c8422dc99ed2098b0f8ab9eaa0d3c82f79988569e8907e1d9`, equal to the review. The product is unchanged and uncommitted.

Findings:

- **R1 (blocking)**: the heavy faulted CMake rerun stopped at its first check after takeover. `cmake_debug` and `cmake_release` both failed
  `ledger format initializes exact metadata schema` because the gitignored, untracked `tests/native/r12b-evidence/expected-schema.sql`
  was missing from the export. `stopped_result` was returned yet `run_heavy` exited 0; there was no reject, repair, `max_rounds`, approval or replay.
  `index.json` (L20) and limits/packet/index/scan/verify-redaction (L21) were not run. The "MET" statements in the acceptance table and the
  `r19-accept-evidence/README.md` A1 note are wrong and must be corrected. C01-R1 stays open. Whether the missing schema is a harness
  export-composition defect or a repository defect is a decision for C02-fix; the review states only the observed cause. `run_heavy` must
  exit nonzero on `stopped_result`.
- **R2 (blocking)**: `COMMIT-LIST.txt` names the missing `probes/PROBES-reprobe.md` and omits the C02 `REPORT.md` and `probes/PROBES.md`; the
  redaction check was never run on the final list and exited 0 with a missing path; two listed files pin sha256 values of REPORT files (hash-pin rule).
- **R3 (blocking)**: the F11-b/L17 fixture `test_trace_is_derived_from_executed_steps_in_plain_cmake_run` returns through `stopped_result` and never
  reaches `finish()`, so it passes under the mutant; row L17 has no detecting test.
- **A1 (advisory)**: two citations name non-existent paths (`r19-accept-a-c01-evidence/red-c01/history/RED.md` is under `r19-accept-a-evidence`; `gate/suites/summary.txt` is `summary.txt.raw`).
- **A2 (advisory)**: the regenerated 134-id Python after-list is not saved (no test removed, 26 added, per the reviewer).
- **A3 (advisory)**: F-row kills fire through other driver self-checks (for example F4 by the guidance check); rows should name the check that fired.

Closure of C01: R1 not closed, R2 partly, R3 closed except F11-b/L17. Sections above that state the heavy run or the commit list as met are superseded by this outcome.
Next: corrective round R19-ACCEPT-A-C03 (retain heavy-run scratch results under the evidence tree; fix the export composition; stop-rule exit code; real heavy run with
reject/repair/max_rounds, then L20/L21 steps; non-hollow F11-b/L17 fixture; rebuild and recheck COMMIT-LIST; fix A1-A3).

## Dispatch R19-ACCEPT-A-C03 (coordinator, 2026-11-12)

Third corrective for R19-ACCEPT-A, closing the blocking findings R1, R2 and R3 and the advisories A1 to A3 of the
[C02 review](R19-ACCEPT-A-C02-review.md). Two request-changes reviews exist in the lineage (C01 and C02), both making progress.
The C02 candidate is kept whole: the history fix in `src/engine/ledger/storage.cpp` with its three tests in `execution.t.cpp`, the
quota combined, split and regroup runs, the mini-fault run, and the F, L and Q probe results all stand. No operator question is open
(Q17 is answered); `remaining-steps-design.md` has no R19-specific acceptance section beyond the R19 table row (single-root
external-driver demonstration under the full matrix), which this round continues.

Starting revision: `91e0df24f2167471e49ec057c7159bce5dff3608` on `dev/planar-v2-vernacular`. The tree is uncommitted. Pre-existing
changes: `roadmap.md` (status edit), `src/engine/ledger/storage.cpp`, `src/engine/ledger/execution.t.cpp`, this record, the C01 and C02
reviews, `tests/native/r19-accept-support/`, and the gitignored evidence folders (`r19-accept-evidence`, `r19-accept-a-evidence`,
`r19-accept-a-c01-evidence`, `r19-accept-a-c02-evidence`). Candidate identity must come from the workflow identity tool only.

### Objective, in strict order

1. R1 (first; nothing later starts until it is done). The cause is a repository defect: the committed test `ledger format initializes
   exact metadata schema` (`src/engine/ledger/CMakeLists.txt:41`, definition `PLANAR_LEDGER_SCHEMA`) reads
   `tests/native/r12b-evidence/expected-schema.sql`, which is gitignored (`.gitignore:37`, `/tests/native/*-evidence/`) and untracked, so
   any clean clone or export fails it (ctest 612). Fix: move the fixture, byte-identical, to a tracked non-evidence location following the
   repository convention (inputs live under `fixtures/`, for example `fixtures/ledger/expected-schema.sql`; there is no `tests/fixtures/`),
   point `PLANAR_LEDGER_SCHEMA` at it, and leave the old evidence copy untouched. Red first, behavioural: show the clean-export failure of
   that test before the fix (exact command, hash of the pre-fix snapshot) and its pass after, from a fresh `git archive`-style export plus
   the untracked-unignored files. Explain in the record why the Linux gate passed: `scripts/linux-gate.sh` (`write_manifest`, lines 125 to
   129) builds its context from `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` and then appends
   `tests/native/r12b-evidence/expected-schema.sql` explicitly, so the ignored file was silently included. Remove that special case so the
   gate context matches a clean clone, and keep `is_excluded` consistent; check `.dockerignore` and any other list naming the file.
   Then make `run_heavy.py` exit nonzero whenever it returns `stopped_result` (with a test that fails on a zero exit). Then RE-RUN the heavy
   faulted CMake run to completion with the fixed candidate (`planar_authoring_stack` built from the current `src`, explicit path, recorded
   through `accept_lib.require_recorded_candidate`) on a fresh scratch ledger: interrupted check, process restart, `check-recover`,
   takeover, one rejecting verdict, repair, `max_rounds` and blockers, final exact-target approval, byte-identical replays, no check
   re-executed on replay, no ledger writes from inspection. Produce `runs/cmake-rerun/index.json` under
   `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/` through `run_acceptance.py rerun-index` (L20) and run the `limits`, `packet`, `index`, `scan` and
   `verify-redaction` steps (L21). Long runs go in the background with a DONE marker and repeated foreground waits; never return before
   DONE. Keep first-attempt logs and every raw log. If a run exposes another product defect, apply the stop rule: report it with a
   red-first test proposal, do not mark MET, and do not change `src/` beyond the fixture path without a coordinator decision. A stopped
   run is NOT MET. Retain the heavy-run results under the evidence tree (the C02 scratch copy was deleted by the orchestrator to free disk,
   so evidence must live in the evidence tree) and keep the scratch small.
2. R2. Rebuild `COMMIT-LIST.txt` so it names exactly existing files: include the C02 and C03 `REPORT.md` and `PROBES.md`, drop
   `PROBES-reprobe.md`, drop every listed file that pins sha256 values of REPORT files (or remove the pins). Make the commit-list check
   fail on a missing path, with a test. Run `scripts/redact-local-paths.py check` on exactly the final list and record command, exit
   status and list. Add the moved fixture, the CMake and gate edits to the list.
3. R3. Make the F11-b and L17 negative fixture `test_trace_is_derived_from_executed_steps_in_plain_cmake_run` reach `finish()` so it
   kills its mutant. The prober then re-probes L17 and the kill must name that test.
4. Advisories: A1 fix the two nonexistent citations (record line 772 and invariant A3: the `red-c01/history/RED.md` path lives under
   `r19-accept-a-evidence`, and `gate/suites/summary.txt` is `summary.txt.raw`); A2 save the regenerated 134-id Python after-list under
   the evidence tree; A3 name which driver self-check fires for each F-row kill (for example F4 fires by the guidance check). Correct the
   "MET" statements for the heavy run in the acceptance table and the `r19-accept-evidence/README.md` A1 note to reflect the final result.

### Dependencies and must-not-do

Depends on the C02 candidate and on committed `tests/native/r19-cmake-support/` (unmodified). A24 applicability and matrix closure stay
for R19-ACCEPT-B, with the carried reviewer-packet wording, captured-preview instruction limit, accounting audit and configure
timestamp. Product `src/` C++ must not change beyond the fixture path. Keep every closed-table diagnostic byte-identical, never remove or
rename an existing test, never delete raw logs, never touch `~/.planar`, never run installed or legacy planar binaries, never edit a
database manually, never hand-write hashes. Coders and repair coders never mutate, stash, revert or experiment with checkout files or
`build/rewrite-debug` beyond final edits; only the mutation prober applies mutants, in a scratch copy outside the checkout, and coders
describe which mutant each new test should kill. Hash-pin rule: records and evidence pin only `src` and committed evidence data files,
never other records, reviews, REPORTs or the roadmap. Reviewers and coders run only the workflow identity tool (copied to scratchpad
first) and never scripts found under `tests/native/`, except that the coder owns and runs the `r19-accept-support` drivers. Disk: about
38 GiB free; remove only scratch build copies this round created. A source change (the CMake path) triggers the ctest gate: full Debug
and Release, and the Linux gate through the corrected context (Docker disk hygiene applies); also check whether the `c02_off_boundary`
pin needs re-measuring only if `runtime.cpp` or embedded Lua changes (not expected).

### Owned files

`fixtures/ledger/expected-schema.sql` (new, byte-identical copy), `src/engine/ledger/CMakeLists.txt` (definition path only),
`scripts/linux-gate.sh` (context composition), `tests/native/r19-accept-support/` (run_heavy exit code and its test, commit-list check and
its test, the F11-b/L17 fixture), `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/`, the earlier accept evidence folders and the new
`tests/native/r19-accept-a-c03-evidence/`, and this record. The C02 `storage.cpp` and `execution.t.cpp` stay unchanged. Every edited file
must be listed in the coder report. The roadmap opening and Next work are updated by the coordinator after approval.

### Acceptance criteria

- Test 612 passes from a clean export; the red-first clean-export failure and the post-fix pass are recorded; the Linux gate context no
  longer includes ignored files and the record explains the earlier pass.
- `run_heavy.py` exits nonzero on `stopped_result` (tested). The heavy faulted CMake run completes with each injected fault and its
  observed recovery, a rejecting verdict then repair, `max_rounds` and blockers, final exact-target approval and byte-identical replays;
  `runs/cmake-rerun/index.json` exists (L20); `limits`, `packet`, `index`, `scan`, `verify-redaction` pass (L21).
- `COMMIT-LIST.txt` names exactly existing files, the check fails on a missing path (tested), no REPORT hash pins remain, and the
  redaction check exits 0 on exactly that list.
- L17 is detected by the named test after re-probe; all other probe results stand.
- Advisories A1 to A3 resolved; no `.pyc` in the support tree.

### Agreed verification

Strict wave order: R1 (fixture move, red-first, heavy-run exit code, heavy re-run, L20, L21), R2, R3, mutation re-probe of L17 and the
F-row kill-source sweep, ctest Debug and Release plus the Linux gate, record sync, Opus review. On approval the coordinator commits
R19-ACCEPT-A, C01, C02 and C03 together (including the `storage.cpp` fix and its tests and the fixture move) with explicit paths, `git add
-f` for gitignored evidence, `git commit -s`, no co-author trailer, never raw logs or any file failing redaction, then updates the
roadmap opening and Next work.

### Gate scope reconciliation (C3-D32) and the Docker and Release deviation

(a) The dispatch asked for Debug and Release ctest and the Linux gate. (b) The live operator answer of 2026-11-11 limits gates before the final one to Debug, and C3-D17 therefore ordered
Debug ctest only, no Docker command, and `docker builder prune -f` as the only Docker disk command, with the Release ctest and the Linux gate carried to the final gate of R19-ACCEPT-B; C3-D17 adds that
keeping the dispatch full gate "becomes an operator question". (c) What happened: the final gate run (`raw/run3/`) ran Debug and Release ctest AND the Linux gate through Docker, and pruned with
`docker builder prune -af --filter 'description~=home/planar'` (the filtered `-a` form, not the plain `-f` form). I find no recorded operator or coordinator decision that lifted C3-D17, so this is
a DEVIATION from C3-D17, recorded as such and not approved here. All of it passed (1854 of 1854 on each configuration, prune freed 0 B) and it is reported as extra evidence only: it does not replace the final gate of R19-ACCEPT-B, and the carry of Release and Linux to B stays in force. Whether to accept the deviation is an operator
question for the review; no decision is made in this sync. Before the final gate, no Docker command was run (PKG-R1 evidence `r1/RESULT.md` says the same for its own steps); the Linux evidence of the earlier Debug-only
PKG-GATE was the static recipe checks (`x08.linux_recipe_pins`, greps over the three recipe files, the git-list manifest check, the ignored-file observer; `gate/linux/`: `r12b-evidence` grep exit 1,
re-include grep exit 1, Dockerfile `test -s fixtures/ledger/expected-schema.sql` at line 90, manifest holds the fixture once and the old path zero times, ignored-file count 0). macOS arm64 and Linux arm64 only; x86_64 Linux is uncovered.

### Why the Linux gate passed with the ignored schema file (C3-R1j)

Before the fix the schema lived only at the gitignored, untracked path `tests/native/r12b-evidence/expected-schema.sql`. The Linux gate context still held
it because four sites named the old path, each cited at its pre-fix line:

- `write_manifest` in `scripts/linux-gate.sh` (lines 125-129) appended the old path with an explicit `printf '%s\0'` after the `git ls-files` list.
- The `is_excluded` case block in `scripts/linux-gate.sh` (lines 101-104) returned 1 (not excluded) for the old path, ahead of the `*-evidence` exclusions.
- The dockerignore re-include `!tests/native/r12b-evidence/expected-schema.sql` in `scripts/linux-gate.Dockerfile.dockerignore` (line 47) put the file back after
  the `tests/native/*-evidence/**` exclusion.
- The Dockerfile `RUN test -s tests/native/r12b-evidence/expected-schema.sql` in `scripts/linux-gate.Dockerfile` (line 91) asserted the file in the image.

Any one of the four missing would have failed or emptied the context; together they made the Linux gate pass while a public clone (without the ignored file)
would fail. All four are removed or repointed to `fixtures/ledger/expected-schema.sql` (the Dockerfile assertion now sits at line 90); red-first and observer evidence is under `r1/` of
`tests/native/r19-accept-a-c03-evidence/`. Direct observer: `ignored_context_check.sh` prints count 1 with the old path before the fix and count 0 after (`r1/raw/ignored-context-before.txt`, `-after.txt`, `gate/linux/ignored-context.out`).

### Red-first (`red/RED.md`, `red/RED-1.md`, `r1/RED.md`, `red-t/`, `red-t2/`, `red-t3/`)

Method: Python rows `python3 -B -m unittest <module>.<Class>.<test>` from `tests/native/r19-accept-support` against a pre-fix snapshot (`red/snapshot-2/`, tracked diff identity of `src tests/native` recorded in
`red/RED.md`, the reds of `red/snapshot/` in `red/RED-1.md`); C++ and x08 rows `ctest -R <exact name>`; the fixture row in a clean export (`r1/RED.md`: pre-fix identity pasted, NUL export lists before and after hashed,
`grep -zc` 0 before and 1 after, ctest exit 8 at `format.t.cpp:115`, then pass).
- Real behavioural reds: `HeavyDriverTest` R1l, R1n, R1o, R1p (assertion failures on the unfixed code); `x08.linux_recipe_pins` (`no RUN asserts test -s fixtures/ledger/expected-schema.sql`; only the first failing check is reported, each pin fails alone in a scratch copy, `red-t/x08-scratch-progressive.log`);
  the five `commit-list` tests against the stub (`commit-list: not implemented`; the pass guards fail on exit 1); the four `SchemaFixtureContextTest` observers (`red-t/`); SCHEMA612 in the clean export.
- Not red by design: SCHEMA612 in the checkout (guard: the ignored copy existed); `test_run_heavy_exits_zero_and_writes_result_for_a_completed_scenario` (guard); the trace test (green-first, only the prober applies the mutant; `red-t3/NOTE.md`); the repair test for the trajectory survivor (added after probing; no red run, none invented).
- Not runnable at red time: the live `FaultLiveTest` guards need the recorded candidate (`red/RED-1.md`); RECORD rows have no red run.
- Limits: the stage-0 identity slip (item 6a); the PKG-T/PKG-T2 coders' reds are raw logs plus `red-t2/RED.md`, no hash-pinned snapshot for the three small packages; the pre-fix hashes of `accept_test.py` and `x08_recipe.py` were reconstructed (`stage0/NOTE.md`).

### Probe results (`probes/PROBES.md`, revision 2, final)

Mutants were applied only by the prober, in a persistent scratch copy outside the checkout, each restored by byte copy and verified equal; the C++-affecting mutant (CMake path) was rebuilt after restore and SCHEMA612 passed again; the baseline control passes.
- Table 1 holds 81 mutant rows, all KILLED (including RECORD-observer kills, which name no test: the shell observer differs from the baseline). Revision 1 rows are carried into revision 2 where the mutated file and its test file are byte-equal to revision 1 (their raw logs are under `probes/superseded-1/raw/`, including `BASELINE.log`); the 14 revision 2 mutants (`C3-R1l-a` to `-d`, `C3-R1m`, `C3-R1m-c`, `-d`, `C3-R1n-a` to `-c`, `C3-R1o-a` to `-c`, `C3-R1p`) have raw logs under `probes/raw/`.

### Heavy run status and acceptance rows (C3-G9, C3-S1)

The C03 heavy faulted CMake run COMPLETED (exit 0, `trajectory.valid` true, no ABORTED.json, no STOPPED file from the driver, first completed check passed its boundary row, replays byte-identical, takeover, fence, verdict and load as scripted). The run-level counter check is NOT MET:
`counter_lines` 7 against `intended_executions` 8, so `rerun-index` refuses it and `runs/cmake-rerun/index.json` (L20) and the L21 post-run steps (`limits`, `packet`, index dry run and `index`, `scan`, `verify-redaction`) are NOT MET (checked on disk: `runs/cmake-rerun/` holds only the two timeout config files). Cause and proposal:
`runs/STOPPED-HARNESS-HEAVY.md` (a harness formula in `fault_driver.py:982`, not a product defect: check-1 was the interrupted round, whose release check never started, so 7 attempt folders ran against 4 x 2). Raw logs are retained under `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/raw/cmake-rerun-heavy/`.
Decision needed from the coordinator, not taken here: change the formula (count only the attempts started in the interrupted round) or relax the validator; either edit to `fault_driver.py` or `run_acceptance.py` (and a new heavy run, because `fault_driver.py` is a heavy-run input, C3-H11) needs a red-first test first. The C02 acceptance row (heavy run executed) states this result; the C02 sentence about the C02 run is historical and unchanged. The README A1 note states the same.

## Closing fix of R19-ACCEPT-A-C03 (coordinator, 2026-11-12)

The operator asked the coordinator to make the closing fix of C03 directly ("do the closing fix yourself"), instead of
dispatching a C04 round. The fix answers C03-R1, R2 and R3 and records A1 to A3. Evidence lives under
`tests/native/r19-accept-a-c03-evidence/closing/`, `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/raw/cmake-rerun-heavy-close/` (raw, local only),
`tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/runs/cmake-rerun/`, `runs/post-index/`, `limits/` and `index/`.

### Coordinator decisions

- C4-D1 (C03-R1, side that changes): the harness formula changes; the validator stays strict. An interrupted round counts only the
  check that started. `fault_driver._Scenario.intended_executions()` returns
  `(check_rounds - interrupted) * checks_per_round + interrupted`, where `interrupted` counts the attempts with outcome `interrupted`.
  Both former call sites (`stopped_result` and `finish`) use it.
- C4-D2 (C03-R1, exit status): `run_heavy.py` calls `run_acceptance.validate_rerun_result` after a valid trajectory. On
  `accept_lib.AcceptError` it prints `run_heavy: the scenario completed but rerun validation refused its result: ...` and returns 4.
  The existing exit codes stay: 3 for a stopped result.
- C4-D3 (C03-A2): the REPORT-pin rule of `commit-list` is NOT widened. Its limit is stated here: it flags a 64-hex string only on a
  line that also contains `REPORT`. It misses review and roadmap pins and a hash on the line next to a REPORT name. A manual grep
  covers that gap: every 64-hex string within one line of `REPORT`, `-review.md`, `roadmap.md` or `R19-ACCEPT-A.md` in a listed
  non-index file. Widening the rule to adjacent lines would contradict the existing guard in
  `test_commit_list_check_refuses_files_that_pin_report_hashes` (a hash with `REPORT mention` on the next line passes).
- C4-D4 (C02-D28 versus C3-D16): `index` writes `index/<run>/raw/...`, mirroring the raw layout, and C02-D28 and L21 put `index/**` in
  the commit list. The C3-D16 rule refused every path with a `raw` directory component, so it refused about 3300 of the 4466 index
  files. The rule gains one closed allowance, `COMMIT_LIST_INDEX_ALLOWED`: an `.index` file under
  `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/index/` may sit under a `raw` component. Raw logs, non-index files, and index trees elsewhere
  are still refused.
- C4-D7 (found while building the list): the C3-D16 `snapshot` rule tested every path part, file names included. So it refused the index of a source file named `snapshot_value.cpp`, `snapshot_probe.t.cpp` or `snapshot.t.cpp` (10 index files). The rule now tests directory components only, which still refuses the red `snapshot*/` folders it was written for.
- C4-D5 (C03-A1): the operator's 2026-11-11 rule (Debug-only before the final gate, one full gate at the end, and an unchanged
  product candidate cites its gate instead of re-running ctest and Linux) is the reading the coordinator applies. The C03 final
  gate (Debug, Release, clean export and Linux) is therefore the final full gate of R19-ACCEPT-A, not an unauthorized extra run.
  The filtered `-af` prune is the operator's standing post-Linux-gate instruction and removes only build cache of this repository.
- C4-D6 (C03-A3): the hidden C03 export copy `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/.raw/cmake-rerun-heavy-export/` (95 MB) is kept as
  the only retained export source. The closing heavy run did not get a second copy. Its post-run export source list
  (`raw/cmake-rerun-heavy-close/EXPORT-FILES-after-run.sha256`, 6591 files, build directories excluded) is byte-identical to that
  copy's `EXPORT-FILES.sha256` without its own two bookkeeping lines (`diff` empty). Both `.raw/` and `raw/` stay off the commit list.

  The candidate is the recorded C01 rev2 `planar_authoring_stack`, whose sha256 equals `gate/candidate/candidate.json`.

  `raw/cmake-rerun-heavy-close/run-markers/`. Because `run_heavy` now validates, exit 0 means `rerun-index` validation passed inside the run.
- Retained copy: `raw/cmake-rerun-heavy-close/`, which holds every run output except `export/` (10 GB of build trees) and `home/`, the
  same split as C03. It has 903 files, and `SHASUMS-source.sha256` is byte-equal to `SHASUMS-copy.sha256`. The C03 heavy raw
  `raw/cmake-rerun-heavy/` stays as failed raw.
- L20: `python3 -B tests/native/r19-accept-support/run_acceptance.py rerun-index --result tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/raw/cmake-rerun-heavy-close/result.json --out tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/runs/cmake-rerun/index.json`
  exit 0 (`runs/cmake-rerun/rerun-index.{stdout,stderr,exit}`). The index records:
  - counter_lines 7 = intended_executions 7;
  - the interrupt (CLI SIGKILLed, child group dead, reservation in progress) and `check-recover`;
  - reads after the restart by fresh processes;
  - takeover generation 1 to 2, and the old token refused `fenced` (exit 3) with the ledger unchanged;
  - reject 1 admitted with `repair_admitted`, then reject 2 capped with a `max_rounds` blocker;
  - the load raising max_rounds from 1 to 2, which clears it;
  - final approve accepted with `targets_equal_latest_output` true;
  - every replay response- and ledger-identical;
  - the ledger digest equal before and after replays;
  - the trajectory.

### Operator decision at commit: evidence trimmed (2026-11-12)

The operator asked why the commit carried so much evidence, and decided that it follows the evidence rule in
[orchestration](../orchestration.md) (evidence folders are gitignored; commit reports, READMEs and small records of 100 KB or
less; bulky artifacts stay local with their hashes recorded). This overrides C02-D28, C3-D16, L21 and C4-D4 where they put
`index/**` and `TEMP-PATH-REDACTION.json` in the commit. The local commit was amended before any push.

- Kept local (still on disk, gitignored, unchanged): 4480 files. These are the 4466 files under
  `tests/native/r19-accept-evidence/index/`, and 13 other evidence files over 100 KB: the four `inventory.txt` files, the C03 and
  canonical test-name lists, the C03 Linux `manifest.nul`, `runs/post-index/scan-whole-tree.stdout`, `TEMP-PATH-REDACTION.json` and
  the tree-local `tests/native/path-redaction.json`. The fifteenth is the old 4852-entry `COMMIT-LIST.txt`, which was regenerated.
- `tests/native/r19-accept-a-c03-evidence/closing/LOCAL-ONLY.txt` records them: a file count and tree sha256 for each of the nine
  index trees, and bytes and sha256 for each single file, with the recipe for the tree hash. Every single file was checked equal to
  its blob in the pre-amend commit before it was unstaged.
- `COMMIT-LIST.txt` now names exactly the amended commit's files (the earlier list minus the files above, plus `LOCAL-ONLY.txt`).
  `list-checks.sh` was re-run on it; results are in `closing/list/`.
- `COMMIT_LIST_INDEX_ALLOWED` (C4-D4) stays in `run_acceptance.py` with its tests; it now has no effect on the committed list.
- Close-A3 is resolved by this decision.
